| SVC-TZ-05 | SERVICE | any stored or returned instant | format | ends in `Z`; no zone conversion call exists in main code (ARC-024) | user decision | no |

### 6.16a SVC-KEY: session keys (`SessionKeyService`, owner only)
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-KEY-01 | SERVICE | author and an Ed25519 public key | register | bound; answer has the fingerprint (SHA-256 hex of the DER); row has `registered_by=user:owner`, the clock time, the note; another author has no key | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-02 | SERVICE | the key already live for the author | register again (with surrounding whitespace) | nothing changes: same id, one row, note unchanged | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-03 | SERVICE | a live key | `registerReplacing` another key naming the live fingerprint (changed: a plain `register` of another key is refused, SVC-KEY-09) | the old row is closed (`revoked_at` = clock, `revoked:<id>` keys) and stays; the old key signs `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, the new key writes | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-04 | SERVICE | a key live for author A | register it for B | `DUPLICATE_KEY`, B has no key; after A's key is revoked B can register it | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-05 | SERVICE | null, empty, blank, not base64, an RSA key, a private key, `AAAA`; authors `claude`, `user:someone`, null | register | key: `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `public_key` reason `MUST_BE_ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY`; author: `AUTHOR_ID_FORMAT` / `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `author`; nothing registered | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-06 | SERVICE | a live key | revoke twice, and for an author without key | true, false, false; the row stays closed; the author's token is `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` (changed by attest-fix, F5: was `SESSION_KEY_NOT_REGISTERED`) until a key is registered again (then the same token works) | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-07 | SERVICE | two registrations of one author with different keys at the same time (changed: no live key before) | register | exactly one live key and one row; the other call is refused `KEY_REPLACEMENT_NOT_CONFIRMED` after its retry (no silent replacement) | D-0012; SVC-GRT-06 | no |
| SVC-KEY-09 | SERVICE | an author with a live key (registered 2026-11-05T10:00:00Z) | `register` another key; `registerReplacing` with no fingerprint, a blank one, another key's fingerprint, the live fingerprint in upper case | the first three: `KEY_REPLACEMENT_NOT_CONFIRMED` (409) whose args are author, the live fingerprint and the registration time `2026-11-05T10:00:00Z`, or `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `replace` for no/blank fingerprint; nothing changed (still one live row, the old key still signs); the last one replaces (SVC-KEY-03); the same key again with either call changes nothing (SVC-KEY-02) | audit F4 | no |
| SVC-KEY-10 | SERVICE | an owner identity | register, register the same key again, replace, revoke, revoke again; register again as another owner | `owner_audit` rows `KEY_REGISTERED`, none for the idempotent repeat, `KEY_REPLACED` (detail names the new and the replaced fingerprint), `KEY_REVOKED`, none for the second revoke, `KEY_REGISTERED`; actor = the identity of each call | audit D8 | no |
| SVC-KEY-12 | SERVICE | an author whose key was registered and whose grant was refused afterwards (the grant screen registers the key first) | register the same key again (also with `registerReplacing` naming that key's own fingerprint and with another letter case and spaces in the text) | idempotent success: the existing row is answered, not `KEY_REPLACEMENT_NOT_CONFIRMED`, no second key row, no second audit row; so the owner can simply submit the grant again | audit F4 | no |
| SVC-KEY-11 | SERVICE | a public key text | `fingerprintOf` | the fingerprint the owner compares (same as `register` answers); an invalid key is `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `public_key`; nothing is stored | grant screen | no |
| SVC-KEY-08 | SERVICE | register, revoke, register | read the rows | ids from the counter without gaps; rows hold only the public key (60 base64 characters) and the fingerprint | D-0012 | no |
| SVC-KEY-13 | SERVICE | (audit2 S7) the `SessionKeyService` proxy and three registrations of the same key for the same author at the same moment | read the advisors of the proxy; register in parallel | the retry advice is before the transaction advice (retry outer, transaction inner: every attempt starts a transaction of its own, Spring Framework "Retry (OUTER) -> Transaction (INNER)"); all three calls succeed with the same row (a conflict is retried on H2 in a fresh transaction and finds the key that won), one row | audit2 S7 | no |
| SVC-KEY-17 | SERVICE | (found by the build of import: the race behind SVC-KEY-13) an author with no live key; the registration of key K finds no live key with the fingerprint of K, then another registration of K for the same author becomes live before the lookup of the author's live key (a state repository of the test commits it at that fixed point) | `register` of K for the author | the registration answers with the live row (id of the other one): no `KEY_REPLACEMENT_NOT_CONFIRMED`, one `session_key` row, one `owner_audit` row, nothing closed; red on the code before the fix, which refused the presented fingerprint as if it were another key | SVC-KEY-13 | no |
| SVC-KEY-14 | SERVICE | (audit2 S5) author `user:owner` | `register`, `registerReplacing` | `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `author`, reason `MUST_BE_A_SESSION_AUTHOR`; nothing stored, nothing audited | audit2 S5 | no |
| SVC-KEY-15 | SERVICE | (audit2 N3) an author with a live key | `revokeConfirmed(author, fingerprint, actor)` with another fingerprint, none or blank, the right one (spaces, upper case), again, for an author without a key; `liveKeys` | another fingerprint: `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `fingerprint` reason `MUST_MATCH` naming the live fingerprint; none: `REQUIRED`; nothing closed; the right one closes the key (the row stays, `revoked_at` set), `owner_audit` has `KEY_REVOKED` with the actor and `key=<fingerprint>`; again or no key: `NOT_FOUND`; `liveKeys` lists the live keys by author | audit2 N3 | no |
| SVC-KEY-16 | SERVICE | (audit2 S1) a bare public key and the four-line block of the hook for the author | `present(author, text)` | `PresentedKey` with the canonical key, its fingerprint and the server of the block (null for the bare key); a block for another author is `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `author` reason `KEY_BLOCK_AUTHOR_DIFFERS` (naming the block author); a block whose fingerprint line is not its key: path `public_key` reason `KEY_BLOCK_FINGERPRINT_DIFFERS`; a block with a missing line: `MUST_BE_SESSION_KEY_BLOCK`; a bad key in a block: `MUST_BE_ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY`; null and text that is no key: `VALIDATION_FAILED`; nothing is stored | audit2 S1 | no |

### 6.17 SVC-RACE: races
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SVC-RACE-01 | SERVICE | latch-driven interleavings: merge of D5 into D2 starts, record_case on D5 starts (a) while merge waits at its end (b) after merge commit | run both | after (a) and (b): the merge commits; the case is either refused with `MERGED_DEFECT: <D5> was merged into <D2>` or recorded before the merge and then visible as a case of D2; invariant: no case on a MERGED defect has a history id greater than that defect's merge TRANSITION row; no case remains that is invisible to the target. Mechanism (decision 7m): `record_case` runs `UPDATE defect SET case_seq = case_seq + 1 WHERE id = ? AND status <> 'MERGED'`; affected rows 0 -> `MERGED_DEFECT`; the merge updates the same row so the two serialise; `case_seq` does not bump `version` | e2e.sh:S-R01,S-R02,S-R03 | yes |
| SVC-RACE-09 | SERVICE | defect D1 with `version` 3 | 20 `record_case` calls, sequentially and then 20 in parallel | `case_seq` +20, 20 cases, `version` still 3, no defect history row, a concurrent `ledger_save` with `expected_version` 3 still succeeds (the bump does not cause `VERSION_CONFLICT`) | decision 7m | yes |
| SVC-RACE-02 | SERVICE | two transitions with the same expected version | run concurrently | exactly one commits, the other `VERSION_CONFLICT` (never a raw SQL error) | sql 40001 | yes |
| SVC-RACE-03 | SERVICE | two edits with the same expected version | run | same | sql save | yes |
| SVC-RACE-04 | SERVICE | two record_fix on one OPEN defect, different commits | run | defect SOURCE_FIXED, exactly one OPEN->SOURCE_FIXED TRANSITION history row, every successful call has its fix row; losers may get `VERSION_CONFLICT`, nothing else | sql record_fix "no write skew" | yes |
| SVC-RACE-05 | SERVICE | two reviewers verify the same SOURCE_FIXED defect | run | exactly one VERIFIED; the other `VERSION_CONFLICT` or `TRANSITION_INVALID` | new | yes |
| SVC-RACE-06 | SERVICE | two identical links | run | one OK, one `NOTHING_CHANGES`; no duplicate-key leak | sql link | yes |
| SVC-RACE-07 | SERVICE | write while the grant is revoked in parallel | run 200 rounds | never a write committed after the revoke commit; REPO-016 at service level | sql FOR SHARE (rewritten) | yes |
| SVC-RACE-08 | SERVICE | code-set vs save using the new code | run | save uses either old or new code set consistently, no exception other than `CODE_INVALID` | new | yes |
| SVC-RACE-10 | SERVICE | F9: two transactions, tx A runs the gate of a write (the key check passed, not committed), tx B closes the key of the author (revoke) | latch-driven: B starts after A verified; then A commits; then repeat with B committed first | B waits for A's commit (the close does not overtake a verified write); after A committed, B closes; a write that verifies after B committed is `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; 200 rounds with a write and a revoke in parallel: never a write committed after the revoke commit (the same as SVC-RACE-07 for grants) | audit F9 | yes |

## 7. MCW: MCP write-tool contract (MCP Java SDK client -> Boot server, H2)

Ten write tools: `ledger_save, ledger_transition, ledger_record_fix, ledger_record_case, ledger_link, ledger_correct, ledger_batch, ledger_review, ledger_review_correct, ledger_whoami`. Tool names, input keys, `_hostAttestation` and error tokens are the external contract.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCW-001 | MCP | started server | `tools/list` | contains all ten write tools; contains no tool named `execute_sql*` or `search_objects*` (writing or reading) | e2e.mjs:E-01,E-02 | no |
| MCW-002 | MCP | each write tool schema | inspect | `_hostAttestation` is an optional string (not in `required`); `author_id` is not a declared property; unknown properties are not allowed | e2e.mjs:H1 | no |
| MCW-003 | MCP | each of the ten tools | call without `_hostAttestation` | `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED` for save, whoami and the other eight (transition, record_fix, record_case, link, correct, batch, review, review_correct); nothing written | e2e.mjs:H2 (both blocks) | no |
| MCW-004 | MCP | whoami with `_hostAttestation=AUTHOR` and `author_id=NOGRANT` | call | `author` is AUTHOR and `grants` has the 4 AUTHOR grants: legacy `author_id` accepted and ignored | e2e.mjs:H3 | no |
| MCW-005 | MCP | save with only legacy `author_id=AUTHOR` | call | `HOST_ATTESTATION_REQUIRED` | e2e.mjs:H3 | no |
| MCW-006 | MCP | arguments `titel`, `authorId`, `Author_Id`, `_hostattestation`, extra nested key in `fields` | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming each unknown argument; `author_id` is the only accepted legacy key; `_hostAttestation=""` -> `AUTHOR_ID_FORMAT` | plan: unknown argument rejected | no |
| MCW-007 | MCP | `fields` as JSON object and as legacy JSON string with the same content | call both with the same `request_id` | identical effect; second is a receipt replay (same normalised digest) | plan: object and legacy string | no |
| MCW-008 | MCP | `ops` as JSON array and as legacy JSON string | `ledger_batch` | identical results | plan | no |
| MCW-009 | MCP | `fields` as invalid JSON string, `"[]"`, `"7"`, `"null"`, `null`, `{}`; `ops` as `"{"`, `{}`, `[]` | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED`, nothing written | plan; sql save/batch | no |
| MCW-010 | MCP | whoami with granted attestation | call | keys `session_user` (login label of the channel, P), `author`, `author_format_ok=true`, `can_verify_login=false`, `grants[]{project,scope,expires_at}` | e2e.mjs:E-06 | no |
| MCW-011 | MCP | whoami with ungranted / malformed attestation | call | ungranted: empty `grants`; malformed (`claude`): `author_format_ok=false` | sql whoami | no |
| MCW-012 | MCP | all results of write tools | inspect response | `structuredContent` is the result record itself (keys as in SVC tables); no `rows[0].result` wrapper; text content carries the same JSON; `isError=false` | plan: remove DBHub wrapper; e2e.mjs `result()` helper | no |
| MCW-013 | MCP | a failing call | inspect | `isError=true`, `structuredContent.code` = token, `details`, text content begins `<TOKEN>: `; message localised by `MessageSource`; no SQL, class names or stack traces | plan: error conversion | no |
| MCW-014 | MCP | `ledger_transition` input schema | inspect `to` | enum of author-channel states only (no VERIFIED, MET, NOT_MET); `ledger_review` `to` includes them; sending VERIFIED to `ledger_transition` -> `VALIDATION_FAILED` | e2e.mjs:E-16; K6 | no |
| MCW-015 | MCP | `ledger_review` without `evidence_uri` | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `evidence_uri` (structural, Jakarta group is the source of truth) | e2e.mjs:V3 | no |
| MCW-016 | MCP | enum schemas | inspect | `entity` = defect/task/condition; `ci_conclusion` = success/failure/cancelled; `ledger_link.kind` = `defect_task, condition_task, condition_defect` + active RELATION_KIND codes in `sort_order` | e2e.mjs:K2,K6,K8 | no |
| MCW-017 | MCP | `ledger_review` description | read | states CI is optional metadata, the reviewer runs the real program directly, and points to the `fixed_by` field of `ledger_defect` | e2e.mjs:V1 (rewritten: `v_defect.fixed_by`) | no |
| MCW-018 | MCP | `expected_version` as `"3"`, 3.5, -1; `request_id` not UUID; `reason` of 9 chars | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` / `REASON_INVALID` (no numeric-string coercion) | plan: structural validation | no |
| MCW-019 | MCP | AUTHOR-channel tools vs REVIEWER-channel tools | call verify transition through `ledger_transition` with `_hostAttestation` of a verifier | `VERIFY_NEEDS_REVIEWER_LOGIN` (or schema rejection); through `ledger_review` ok; `ledger_correct` of a verify row refused, `ledger_review_correct` allowed | e2e.sh:S-D01 | no |
| MCW-020 | MCP | injected failure inside a tool call after the first insert | call | whole call rolled back (proxy-applied `@Transactional` and validation active regardless of the callback path) | plan: tool through service proxy (spike c) | no |
| MCW-021 | MCP | 20 parallel sessions with different attestations | call whoami/save | each result's `author` equals the caller's attestation | plan: ScopedValue | no |
| MCW-022 | MCP | body above the configured limit | call | HTTP 413 / `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, nothing written, server keeps serving | plan (resource limit) | no |
| MCW-023 | MCP | `ledger_save` input schema | inspect `fields` | typed properties per the create/edit groups; `category` and `severity` carry the active codes as enum + description (P path `fields.category.enum`) | e2e.mjs:K1 | no |
| MCW-024 | MCP | tools invoked as `ledger_get`-style read tool with `_hostAttestation` | call | read tools do not need it; property is not declared (see MCR-001) | plan | no |
| MCW-026 | MCP | body above the configured limit sent with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (no `Content-Length`), and a chunked body exactly at the limit | call | above: HTTP 413 / `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (problem detail), nothing written, the body is not read beyond the limit + 1 byte; at the limit: accepted. One servlet filter (counts the bytes, rejects through the `HandlerExceptionResolver` to the one advice) also answers the declared `Content-Length` case of MCW-022; Tomcat `maxPostSize` (form parsing) and `maxSwallowSize` (aborted uploads) do not apply | security audit (Tomcat 11 HTTP connector documentation) | no |
| MCW-027 | MCP | `ledger_batch` with `ops:[{"op":"drop"}]` and with an op that has no `op` key | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED`; unknown op: `UNKNOWN_VALUE` at `ops[0].op` with the allowed ops (`save`, `transition`, `record_fix`, `record_case`, `link`, `correct`); missing: `REQUIRED` at `ops[0].op`; neither the text nor the structured content has `org.pgledger` or `BatchOp`; other Jackson failures carry a fixed reason, not the library text | security audit: internal class name in the error result | no |
| MCW-030 | MCP | each of the ten tools; the attacker holds the bearer token only and knows an author id from `ledger_history` | call with `_hostAttestation` = the bare author id (the old format), sent without the hook | `ATTESTATION_UNSIGNED`, nothing written (red before D-0012: the write succeeded under the other session's name) | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-031 | MCP | each of the ten tools | forged signature (any key pair); signature made with the key of another registered author | `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, nothing written, the token is not in the error | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-032 | MCP | each of the ten tools | token signed for another tool | `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH`, `details.field=tool` | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-033 | MCP | each of the ten tools | token signed for another request id, or for none (whoami: for any id) | `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH`, `details.field=request_id` | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-034 | MCP | save | issue time 600 s / 1 day before and after the server time; 240 s either side | outside: `ATTESTATION_TIME_WINDOW` with `window_seconds=300`; inside: written | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-035 | MCP | author with a grant and no registered key | save | (changed by attest-fix, F5: was `SESSION_KEY_NOT_REGISTERED` naming the author) `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; the message names `session-key` and no author; see MCW-043 | D-0012; F5 | no |
| MCW-036 | MCP | key revoked, then another registered | save | (changed by attest-fix, F5) revoked: `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` (was `SESSION_KEY_NOT_REGISTERED`); old key: `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID`; new key writes | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-037 | MCP | a signed save | the captured call again; other content under its request id; its token with another request id | stored response; (changed by attest-fix, F3: was `REQUEST_CONFLICT`) `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` `details.field=arguments` (the signature covers the content, so other content is refused before the receipt is looked at; `REQUEST_CONFLICT` stays for the service level and for a token that is valid for both contents, which only the owner of the key can make); `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH`; one history row | D-0012; F3 | no |
| MCW-038 | MCP | whoami | signed; key not registered; bare author; malformed `claude` | grants of the author; (changed by attest-fix, F5) `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` for a key that is not registered (was `SESSION_KEY_NOT_REGISTERED`); `ATTESTATION_UNSIGNED`; `author_format_ok=false` | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-039 | MCP | save | `pgla2.` (changed by attest-fix: was `pgla1.`) with a wrong shape, bad encoding, 5000 characters; a `pgla1.` token | `ATTESTATION_MALFORMED`, the text is not in the error; the `pgla1.` token: `ATTESTATION_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED` | D-0012; F1/F3 | no |
| MCW-041 | MCP | F1: a captured signed `ledger_batch` (token for the batch request id and arguments) of an author with grants | the token sent again over the wire with new op request ids; with an extra op; without the first op; after the legitimate batch committed; and the legitimate batch | the variants: `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` `details.field=arguments`, `op_index` 1, no history row more; the legitimate batch writes once and its replay returns the stored results | audit F1 | no |
| MCW-042 | MCP | F3: each of the nine write tools, a signed call | the token with one changed argument (a field, an id, a reason, an added optional key); with `fields` as an object where the token was made for the JSON text (and the reverse); with the keys in another order and other whitespace (JSON text of `fields`) | changed content and the other form: `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` `details.field=arguments`; another key order of the same content: accepted | audit F3 | no |
| MCW-043 | MCP | F5: author without key, author with key, well-formed token with a zero signature | each of the ten tools | one identical error body (token, message, details, no author, no key text) for the unregistered author and for the registered one | audit F5 | no |
| MCW-044 | MCP | (audit2 N2) the token of a `ledger_save` signed for the decimal `expected_version` `9007199254740992.0` | the same call with the integer `9007199254740993` and with `9007199254740992` under that token | `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH` with `details.field = arguments` for both (the server does not verify an integer beyond 2**53-1, so the digest never matches), nothing written; before audit2 the two integers had the digest of the decimal and passed the gate | audit2 N2 | no |
| MCW-040 | MCP | the built hook jar (process) and the MCP Java SDK client | `session-key <server> <author>` (changed by attest-fix: takes the server name) creates and prints the key; the owner registers it and grants the session; the hook signs a save and a whoami event as Claude Code would send them; the client sends exactly the answer | the save and whoami succeed as that author; the same signed call is idempotent; another `request_id` is `ATTESTATION_BINDING_MISMATCH`; other content under the id is `REQUEST_CONFLICT`; one history row | D-0012 | no |
| MCW-025 | MCP | the MCP scenario of the old suite, executed in order with the same data (E-07..E-28, C1..C15, P1..P5, V1..V7 through the tools) | run | every step has the outcome listed in the SVC cases it maps to | e2e.mjs whole file | no |

## 8. MCR: MCP read-tool contract (12 tools)

Tools: `ledger_get, ledger_defect, ledger_task, ledger_history, ledger_search, ledger_defects, ledger_tasks, ledger_conditions, ledger_attention, ledger_stats, ledger_projects, ledger_codes`. Input/output keys other than `max_rows`, `cursor`, `truncated`, `next_cursor`, `include_before` are provisional `(P)`; each case's semantic is binding. List tools return `items[]`, `truncated`, `next_cursor`.

### 8.1 Common behaviour
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MCR-001 | MCP | the 12 read tools | inspect schemas | each declares `outputSchema`; `structuredContent` validates against it; none declares `_hostAttestation` or `author_id`; unknown args rejected | plan: `generateOutputSchema` | no |
| MCR-002 | MCP | list tools (`ledger_history, ledger_search, ledger_defects, ledger_tasks, ledger_conditions, ledger_attention, ledger_projects, ledger_codes`) | `max_rows` = 1 and 1000 | accepted | plan | no |
| MCR-003 | MCP | same | `max_rows` = 0, -1, 1001, "a", 50.5 | `VALIDATION_FAILED` path `max_rows` | plan | no |
| MCR-004 | MCP | 120 rows | no `max_rows` | 50 items, `truncated=true`, `next_cursor` present | plan: default 50 | no |
| MCR-005 | MCP | exactly `max_rows` rows exist | list | `truncated=false`, `next_cursor` absent/null | plan: setMaxRows(n+1) | no |
| MCR-006 | MCP | `max_rows+1` rows exist | list | `truncated=true`, `next_cursor` present, `items.length = max_rows` | plan | no |
| MCR-007 | MCP | each list tool, 137 rows, `max_rows` 10 | follow `next_cursor` until absent | every row exactly once, stable order, last page `truncated=false` | plan: cursor | no |
| MCR-008 | MCP | page 1 fetched, then 5 new rows inserted and 5 existing rows edited | fetch page 2.. | no duplicate, no skipped original row (cursor stability); new rows appear only where the sort order puts them (newest-first lists: not in later pages; history by id ascending: at the end) | plan: cursor stability | no |
| MCR-009 | MCP | garbage, truncated, other tool's cursor, cursor with changed filters | call | `VALIDATION_FAILED` (P: cursor bound to tool + filters) | DOM-031 | no |
| MCR-010 | MCP | any read call | compare row counts, versions, `updated_at` before/after 100 calls | unchanged (`@Transactional(readOnly=true)`); no attestation required; no grant needed | e2e.mjs:E-31,E-32 (rewritten) | no |
| MCR-011 | MCP | server | call `execute_sql_reader`, `search_objects_reader`, `ledger_codes` | the first two: unknown-tool protocol error with no side effect; `ledger_codes` exists (the old "removed" check is inverted, see section 18) | e2e.mjs:K1,E-31 | no |
| MCR-012 | MCP | timestamps in any output | inspect | every instant ends with `Z` | user decision 4 | no |
| MCR-013 | MCP | no bearer / wrong bearer | any read tool | HTTP 401 | plan | no |

## 11. CLI: `ledger-cli`

Commands (enum, `ApplicationArguments`): `migrate, project-add, code-set, codes, grant, revoke, grants, secret-set, import-legacy, mcp-check, driver-register`. Every command except `driver-register` needs a registered driver (DRV-012). Exit codes: 0 success, 1 failure, 2 usage.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |

| CLI-004 | CLI | migrated DB | `migrate` again | exit 0, nothing applied, data digest unchanged | e2e.sh:S-I01..S-I03 | yes |

## 12. SEC: secret store (`ledger-cli secret-set`, configtree)

File locations are test directories (configurable); POSIX permission assertions run on Linux hosts, other hosts mark them NOT_RUN honestly.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-001 | CLI | no secrets directory | `secret-set <name>` with hidden input | directory created with mode 0700; file `<name>` created with mode 0600 (via `Files` + `PosixFilePermissions`, mode set at creation, never widened and narrowed later); content equals the input byte for byte | plan: secret store | no |
| SEC-002 | CLI | the same name again | `secret-set` | replaced atomically (temp file 0600 + move); mode stays 0600; no window with wider mode | plan | no |
| SEC-003 | CLI | input source is not a console (piped stdin), or `Console` unavailable | `secret-set` | refuses (exit 1) with a message; never reads the value from arguments, environment or a pipe; the value is not echoed | plan: `Console.readPassword` | no |
| SEC-004 | CLI | commands `secret-get`, `secret-show`, `secret-list`-with-values | run | unknown command (exit 2); no code path prints a stored secret; `secret-set` prints only the name and path | plan: no get | no |
| SEC-005 | CLI | names `../x`, `a/b`, empty, uppercase, 200 chars, `.hidden` | `secret-set` | refused; only `^[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}$` accepted (P) | plan | no |
| SEC-006 | CLI | existing directory with mode 0755 or file with 0644 | `secret-set` | refuses and names the path/mode (P: fail closed); never silently keeps loose permissions | plan | no |
| SEC-007 | SERVICE | secrets directory with files `ledger.mcp.bearer-token` style names (P) | start app with `spring.config.import=configtree:<dir>` | properties bound into `@ConfigurationProperties` records; missing required secret fails startup naming the property, never a value | plan | no |
| SEC-008 | SERVICE | running app | inspect `/actuator/*`, logs, error responses, thread dumps in logs | the secret value never appears; Actuator `env`/`configprops` not exposed | plan | no |
| SEC-009 | E2E | rotate a secret with `secret-set`, restart | call MCP with old and new bearer | old refused, new accepted | plan | no |
| SEC-010 | E2E | `install` on a host that has the old env keys (`DBHUB_AUTH_TOKEN`, admin token, DB passwords) | run installer | values moved into the secret store (0600), env file keeps only references, old env backed up 0600, nothing printed | plan: env -> secrets | no |
| SEC-011 | E2E | unit file and env file after install | scan | no secret-looking literal (32+ hex/base64 strings, DSN with password) | plan | no |
| SEC-012 | CLI | a file system with neither POSIX permissions nor ACLs (a zip file system in the test) | `secret-set` / the store | fails with an explicit message (`NON_POSIX_FILESYSTEM`) instead of storing with default permissions, and creates nothing. Changed by install: a file system with ACLs but without POSIX permissions (the Windows file systems) is no longer refused, it is protected by an ACL (SEC-016 .. SEC-021) | plan; install: Windows | no |
| SEC-013 | CLI | secrets directory or target file owned by another user than the running process (e.g. created by root while the services run as the service account) | `secret-set` | refused before the prompt, exit 1 `FOREIGN_OWNER`, message names path, owner and current user and advises `sudo -u <service account> ledger-cli secret-set <name>`; nothing written | main review of 00f8324: services read the configtree as the service account | no |
| SEC-014 | CLI | parent of the secrets directory (`/etc/<instance>`) missing or not a directory | `secret-set` | refused before the prompt, exit 1 `PARENT_MISSING` naming the parent; only the secrets directory itself is ever created (the installer creates the parent) | main review of 00f8324 | no |
| SEC-015 | SERVICE | a temporary file left in the secrets directory by an interrupted `secret-set` | start an app with `spring.config.import=configtree:<dir>` | the leftover is not bound as a property: temporary names start with `..` (Boot 4.1.2 configtree reads single-dot names but skips `..` names; a characterization test pins this) | main review of 00f8324 | no |
| SEC-016 | CLI | a default file system with ACLs and without POSIX permissions (the test is skipped on any other file system: the capability decides, never the name of the operating system, ARC-033); no secrets directory | `secret-set <name>` (store and command) | the directory and the file are created with an ACL whose every entry is an ALLOW entry of their owner (the current user), holding read, write and delete rights, nothing inherited from the parent remains (an ACL that was merged with inherited entries is replaced by the owner-only one before a byte is written); the content is the exact UTF-8 bytes; the output has the name and path and never the value | user decision: install on Windows too | no |
| SEC-017 | CLI | as SEC-016, the same name again | store | replaced through a new file and an atomic move; the new file has the owner-only ACL; no temporary file is left | SEC-002 for ACLs | no |
| SEC-018 | CLI | as SEC-016, an existing directory whose ACL also allows another account (SYSTEM) | `check`, `store` | refused with `LOOSE_PERMISSIONS` naming the directory and the account; the ACL and the content are unchanged (never repaired) | SEC-006 for ACLs | no |
| SEC-019 | CLI | as SEC-016, an existing secret file whose ACL also allows another account | `store` | refused with `LOOSE_PERMISSIONS` naming the file; content unchanged | SEC-006 for ACLs | no |
| SEC-020 | CLI | as SEC-016, a directory owned by an account other than the current process user | `store` | refused with `FOREIGN_OWNER` naming the path, the owner and the current user; the group Administrators is not accepted as owner, as in the hook's key store | SEC-013 for ACLs; `SessionKeyStore`, `FileTrust` | no |
| SEC-021 | CLI | the default file system and a zip file system | choose the protection | POSIX permissions when the file system supports them, otherwise ACL, otherwise none; the choice is by the attribute views of the file system | ARC-033 | no |
| SEC-022 | CLI | a secret directory | `exists(<name>)` of the store (used by `token-generate`) | false before, true after the secret is stored; it refuses like `check` (a missing parent is `PARENT_MISSING`, a wrong owner or wider access is refused and never reported as present) | install: keep an existing token | no |

## 13. HK: client hooks (one executable jar, three clients)

Clients: Claude, Codex, Antigravity. One module `ledger-client-hook` builds one executable jar (Jackson inside); the first argument is the client enum `claude|codex|antigravity`; it is launched as `java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar <client>`, reads host JSON on stdin and writes the host's JSON on stdout, stateless. Vendor prefixes: `claude`, `codex`, `gemini`. Client manifests/templates call the same jar with their own argument. (Decision 7m replaces the earlier single-file-source idea; no per-client source files exist.)
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HK-001 | HOOK | each client; each of the 10 write tool names in the client's naming (`mcp__<server>__ledger_<op>`; Antigravity `mcp:<server>:ledger_<op>`) with input containing nested data, Korean text, `__proto__` key, `author_id` | run | output adds `_hostAttestation` = a signed token (ATT-001) whose claims are author `<vendor>:<host id>`, tool `ledger_<op>`, the request id of the call and the issue time of the injected clock, and whose signature verifies with the public key printed for that author; preserves every other field unchanged (deep equal) except `request_id`, which is added when it was absent, null or blank (HK-023); Claude: `updatedInput` only and no auto-approval decision; Codex/Antigravity: allow decision, no permission override | claude-hook:CH-01; host-attestation:S01 | no |
| HK-002 | HOOK | absent legacy `author_id` | run | none is created | claude-hook:CH-02; S12 | no |
| HK-003 | HOOK | cached connector schema that requires `author_id` | run | the existing `author_id` is kept, never copied into `_hostAttestation` | S12 | no |
| HK-004 | HOOK | own `_hostAttestation` in the model input with values null, `""`, false, 0, `{}`, `[]`, the expected value, another id | run | explicit deny mentioning "reserved"; no overwrite/updatedInput | CH-03; S02 | no |
| HK-005 | HOOK | host id missing, null, `""`, number, object, 129 chars, whitespace, `other:id`, `bad:id`, `x\n`, `x\r\n`, Korean | run | deny, no fallback to agent/child id | CH-04; S03 | no |
| HK-006 | HOOK | ids `x`, `a._-Z9`, 128 chars | run | accepted and used as is | CH-07; S04 | no |
| HK-007 | HOOK | read tool names (`ledger_get`, `ledger_history`, `ledger_search`, `ledger_defects`...), `ledger_save_extra`, `run_command`, `ledger_save` without MCP prefix, other server tools | run | no output (Claude: `{}`), input untouched; the new read tools are never stamped | CH-05; S05 | no |
| HK-008 | HOOK | malformed JSON; empty stdin; non-object arguments (null, `[]`, text, 7) | run | Claude: exit 2 on malformed JSON; Codex/Antigravity: explicit deny and exit 0; empty stdin no output | CH-06; S06 | no |
| HK-009 | HOOK | invocations with ids A, B, A | run | each uses the supplied id; no state between runs | S07 | no |
| HK-010 | HOOK | client manifests/templates (`hooks.json`, `plugin.json`) | read | (changed by attest-fix, F2) matcher regex is the structural shape `mcp__<server>__[<prefix>_]ledger_<op>` with the ten known operations (Claude, Codex) and `mcp:[<server><sep>]...ledger_<op>` / `call_mcp_tool` (Antigravity), for any server name incl. isolated sink names, and none of the read tools or of a name that does not end in `ledger_<known op>`; the exact rule (prefix equal to the server segment) is the Java code of HK-032, so the matcher is a superset of what is signed; every command is `java -jar <path>/ledger-client-hook.jar <claude\|codex\|antigravity>` with the right argument and the path exists after install; plugin names | S08; S10; S13 (product part) | no |
| HK-011 | HOOK | Antigravity MCP name candidates `mcp:test:ledger_save`, `mcp:test/ledger_save`, `mcp:test_ledger_save`, `mcp:ledger_ledger_save`, `mcp:test:test_ledger_save` | run | all stamped, for the server `test` / `ledger` (changed by attest-fix, F2: `mcp:ledger_save` names no server, so there is no key to sign with: it passes untouched like any other name) | S09; F2 | no |
| HK-012 | HOOK | Antigravity `call_mcp_tool` envelope with `Arguments` as object and as JSON string, extra keys | run | only `Arguments` overwritten, original type kept, envelope untouched; invalid `Arguments` (null, `[]`, 7, `{`, `"text"`) deny; non-ledger ToolName passes; (changed by attest-fix, F2) the ToolName must be `ledger_<op>` or `<ServerName>_ledger_<op>`, and the ServerName (1 to 100 characters of `A-Za-z0-9._-`) is the server of the key; `app_ledger_ledger_<op>` under another ServerName passes untouched | S11; F2 | no |
| HK-013 | HOOK | Codex namespace `mcp__codex_apps__app_ledger_ledger_<op>` | run | all 10 handled, as before; (changed by attest-fix, F2: the audience is the app, so the key is the one of the server identity `codex_apps.<app>`, here `codex_apps.ledger`, and the owner registers the key printed by `session-key codex_apps.ledger <author>`); `mcp__test__ledger_<op>` and `mcp__test__test_ledger_<op>` are handled too; read tool names, an unknown operation (`app_ledger_ledger_save_extra`), `app_ledger_save`, another pseudo server (`mcp__other__app_ledger_ledger_save`) and a malformed app segment pass untouched; a look-alike app `app_evil_ledger_<op>` is signed for under `codex_apps.evil` with its own key, which never verifies under the key of `codex_apps.ledger` | S10; F2 | no |
| HK-014 | HOOK | JSON with numbers `12345678901234567890` (changed by audit2 N2 (RFC 7493 section 2.2): an integer whose magnitude is beyond 2**53-1 has no exact canonical form, so a call with `12345678901234567890`, `9007199254740992`, `9223372036854775807` or any other such integer is denied `ARGUMENTS_NOT_SIGNABLE`, nothing is signed and no key is created, HK-038; `9007199254740991`, `-9007199254740991`, decimals such as `0.10` and `12345678901234567890.0` and `1e300` are still signed and passed on as sent: RFC 8785 canonicalises a decimal by its double and the server reads it as a double too; the "big integers keep their exact value" expectation of this row no longer holds for integers beyond the range), `1e300` (changed by attest-fix (RFC 8785 §3.2.2.3 and §3.2.2.2): every other number is signed by its double and passed on as the original text, but `1e999` overflows a double and a lone surrogate escape is invalid Unicode, so a call with either is denied `ARGUMENTS_NOT_SIGNABLE` and nothing is signed, HK-038; the lone-surrogate pass-through of the old row no longer holds), `-0`, `0.10`, escapes, surrogate pairs, deep nesting (100 levels), duplicate keys, 1 MB body | run | Jackson-based round trip: numbers keep their exact value (big integers and decimals are not turned into doubles), strings and unicode preserved, unknown fields passed through untouched; no crash; within the hook time budget | new (Jackson fidelity in the hook jar) | no |
| HK-015 | HOOK | JVM default charset and locale changed (`-Dfile.encoding=windows-949`, `LC_ALL=C`) | run with Korean input | output bytes are UTF-8 and identical | new | no |
| HK-016 | HOOK | stdout/stderr | run all above | stdout only JSON, stderr empty, exit 0 (except Claude malformed JSON) | S: `stderr === ''` | no |
| HK-017 | HOOK | cold start of `java -jar` | run | completes inside the host hook time limit (5 s on the test host); budget recorded | host-attestation spawn timeout 5000 | no |
| HK-018 | HOOK | hook jar | inspect contents | only the module's classes, the classes of `org/pgledger/attestation/` (the shared token format, JDK only), Jackson (core, annotations, databind) and Jackson's own dependencies; no Spring, no JDBC driver, no secret or config file; hook code uses no network, process, reflection or JDBC API; file system APIs are used only by the classes of package `org.pgledger.hook.keys` (the key store); `System` is used only by `Main`, which also reads the environment variable `LEDGER_HOOK_KEY_DIR` and the property `user.home` (changed by D-0012: the hook now keeps keys; changed by attest-fix, F8: the key file is read only through one channel opened with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, never with `Files.readAllBytes`; `System` is also used by `Main` for the named property `user.name`) | new | no |
| HK-020 | HOOK | first argument `claude`, `codex`, `antigravity`; `Claude`, `gemini`, empty, missing, two arguments (`session-key` takes exactly a server and an author argument: changed by attest-fix, F2) | run | the three enum values select the matching input/output dialect; any other value or a missing argument exits 2 with the usage message `usage: java -jar ledger-client-hook.jar <claude|codex|antigravity> \| session-key <server> <author>` (changed by attest-fix, F2) on stderr and no stdout; the vendor prefix of the author in the signed token follows the enum (`claude:`, `codex:`, `gemini:`) | decision 7m; D-0012 | no |
| HK-022 | HOOK | each of the 10 tools in every naming variant of the three clients that HK-032 accepts (including `mcp__codex_apps__app_ledger_ledger_<op>`, signed for the server identity `codex_apps.ledger`) | run | the `tool` claim of the token is the canonical `ledger_<op>` whatever the host spelling | D-0012 | no |
| HK-023 | HOOK | `request_id` absent / null / blank / text (spaces, upper case) / number / boolean / object / array; whoami | run | absent, null, blank: a random UUID is put into the arguments as `request_id` and signed; text: kept and signed exactly as it is; any other type: deny `REQUEST_ID_INVALID`, nothing signed; whoami: claim is empty and no `request_id` is added | D-0012 | no |
| HK-024 | HOOK | key folder empty; two authors; two servers; second process | first call for an author on a server; second call; call for another author; call of the same author for another server | the first call creates exactly one key file; later calls use the same key (same public key); another author or another server gets another file and key (changed by attest-fix, F2); nothing else is written anywhere | D-0012 | no |
| HK-025 | HOOK | a file system with the POSIX attribute view (capability, not OS name) | first call | the key folder is `rwx------`, the key file `rw-------`, both from creation (extended by attest-fix, F6: where the ACL view exists, an ACL that grants the owner alone, see HK-035) | D-0012; ARC-033 | no |
| HK-026 | HOOK | POSIX view; key file widened to `rw-r-----`, or the folder to `rwxr-xr-x`; a key file that is a link | call | deny `SESSION_KEY_UNAVAILABLE`, nothing signed, the file unchanged | D-0012 | no |
| HK-027 | HOOK | key file with garbage, the author line of another author, or the private key of another pair | call | deny `SESSION_KEY_UNAVAILABLE`; the file bytes are unchanged; no key material in the answer | D-0012 | no |
| HK-028 | HOOK | environment variable `LEDGER_HOOK_KEY_DIR` set, blank or absent; `user.home` | resolve and run as a process | variable set to an absolute path: that folder (changed by attest-fix, F7: a relative path, `.`, `..` forms are refused with `SESSION_KEY_UNAVAILABLE`, nothing is created relative to the working directory; see HK-036); otherwise `<user.home>/.pg-ledger-mcp/session-keys`; the process test creates the key file under the folder the variable names | D-0012 | no |
| HK-029 | HOOK | `session-key <server> <author>` (changed by attest-fix, F2: was `session-key <author>`) for a new and for an existing pair; invalid authors (`claude`, `user:someone`, empty, 129-character session, space); invalid servers (empty, 101 characters, space, `/`, newline); wrong argument counts | run | four stdout lines `server: ...`, `author: ...`, `public-key: <X.509 DER base64>`, `fingerprint: <SHA-256 hex of the DER>`, exit 0, stderr empty, identical on the second run; invalid input exits 2 with empty stdout and one stderr line | D-0012 | no |
| HK-030 | HOOK | injected clock moved by 90 s between two runs | run | `issued at` of the two tokens differs by 90 s (UTC epoch seconds); both verify | D-0012; principle 4.5 | no |
| HK-031 | HOOK | key file with a known private key text | run stamping and `session-key` | neither stdout nor stderr nor any deny text contains the private key text | D-0012 | no |
| HK-032 | HOOK | F2: tool names of the three hosts for the server `ledger` / `test` / `ledger-test` and the ten known operations; names with the prefix not equal to the server segment (`mcp__a__b_ledger_save`; for Claude Code also `mcp__codex_apps__app_ledger_ledger_save`: the Codex Apps namespace belongs to Codex only, where it is signed for under `codex_apps.<app>`, HK-013), an unknown operation (`ledger_save_extra`, `ledger_get`), a name without server (`ledger_save`, `mcp__ledger_save`, `mcp:ledger_save`), an invalid server segment (empty, `a b`) | run | `mcp__<S>__ledger_<op>` and `mcp__<S>__<S>_ledger_<op>` (Claude Code, Codex), `mcp:<S><:\|/\|_>[<S>_]ledger_<op>` (Antigravity direct) and `call_mcp_tool` with `ServerName` `<S>` and `ToolName` `ledger_<op>` or `<S>_ledger_<op>` are stamped with the server `<S>`; every other name passes untouched | audit F2 | no |

| HK-034 | HOOK | F2: the key file | read the content | has a `server=` line before `author=`; a file whose server line is another server (a copied file) is refused `AUTHOR_MISMATCH`-like (`SERVER_MISMATCH`), nothing signed | audit F2 | no |

Rules come from the plan's "기준" section and the later user decisions. Allow-lists are explicit data in the test (a changed allow-list needs review).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| ARC-002 | ARCH | main classes and Gradle/unit files | scan | no `setAccessible`; no `--add-opens`, `--enable-preview`; no preview feature | plan | no |

| ARC-005 | ARCH | Gradle `runtimeClasspath` of every module | resolve | none of picocli, Spring Shell, zt-exec, JPA/Hibernate, Liquibase, Flyway, Lombok, any JDBC driver | plan; user decisions 1,2 | no |
| ARC-006 | ARCH | main classes | scan | no `System.out`, `System.err`, `printStackTrace`; logging only through SLF4J (no `java.util.logging`, Log4j, Commons Logging direct use) | plan | no |
| ARC-007 | ARCH | main classes | scan | no `System.getenv`, no `@Value`, no OS branching (`os.name`, `File.separator` logic) | plan | no |
| ARC-008 | ARCH | main sources | source scan | no empty `catch`, no catch that only returns a default, no catch of `Exception`/`Throwable` outside the single conversion class and the process entry points | plan: no swallowed exceptions | no |
| ARC-009 | ARCH | modules | dependency check | `ledger-domain` depends on no other module and on no web, Jackson or Spring Web types (only Spring Data mapping annotations); `ledger-application` -> domain and `ledger-attestation`; `ledger-attestation` depends on nothing but the JDK; `ledger-schema` has no code dependency; `mcp-server`, `admin-web`, `cli` -> application + domain; `admin-web` and `mcp-server` do not depend on each other; `cli` has no web dependency; `e2e` only through public clients | plan: module direction | no |

| ARC-011 | ARCH | history aggregate | scan | its repository exposes insert and read only (extends `Repository`, no `CrudRepository`, no delete/update/save-existing method, no `@Modifying` query touching history or `schema_history`); no domain method mutates a `HistoryEntry` | plan: append-only | no |
| ARC-012 | ARCH | main classes | scan | raw SQL (`@Query`, `JdbcClient`, `JdbcTemplate`, SQL string literals) only in classes of the `repository` package | plan: ANSI enforcement (1) | no |

| ARC-014 | ARCH | exceptions | scan | all domain exceptions extend the sealed `LedgerException`; exactly one class converts them to codes with an exhaustive `switch`; MCP result and `@RestControllerAdvice` both call it; no `getMessage().contains(...)` | plan | no |
| ARC-015 | ARCH | main classes | bytecode scan | no `switch` over `String` (string-keyed dispatch) in domain/application; entity, state, kind and scope are enums | plan: no string discrimination | no |
| ARC-016 | ARCH | main classes | scan | exactly one `MessageSource` bean; no user-facing literal in exception constructors (messages come from the bundles) | plan | no |
| ARC-017 | ARCH | configuration | scan | `ledger.*` settings bound only by `@ConfigurationProperties` records | plan | no |
| ARC-018 | ARCH | main classes | scan | request context via `ScopedValue`; no `ThreadLocal`; no non-final static fields, no static mutable collections | plan | no |
| ARC-019 | ARCH | domain/application | scan | no `Instant.now()`, `System.currentTimeMillis()`; time only from the injected `Clock` (`Clock.systemUTC` bean) | plan; user decision 4 | no |

## 16. ANSI: static check of SQL

Scope: (a) every SQL string literal in the `repository` package, (b) every `V{n}__*.sql` file. Matching is token-based (word boundary, case-insensitive, after stripping comments and string literals), so `limit_value` or a string `'top'` is not a violation.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ANSI-001 | ANSI | repository SQL strings | scan for the plan's banned tokens | zero hits for: `LIMIT`, `ROWNUM`, `TOP`, `ILIKE`, `~`/`~*`/`!~` regex operators, `::` casts, `RETURNING`, `ON CONFLICT`, `MERGE`, `ON DUPLICATE KEY`, `FOR UPDATE`, `FOR SHARE`, `WITH RECURSIVE`, `jsonb`/`json_*`/array functions (`unnest`, `array_agg`, `string_agg`), `now()`, `gen_random_uuid()`, `pg_advisory*`, `nextval/currval/NEXT VALUE FOR`, partial-index `WHERE` on `CREATE INDEX`, `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, row-value comparison `(a,b) < (?,?)` | plan: ANSI list | no |
| ANSI-002 | ANSI | every `V{n}__*.sql` | same scan plus DDL rules | zero hits of the ANSI-001 tokens | user decision 1 | no |
| ANSI-003 | ANSI | every V file | scan | no `CREATE TRIGGER`, `CREATE PROCEDURE`, `CREATE FUNCTION`, `CREATE VIEW`, `CREATE SEQUENCE`, `IDENTITY`, `GENERATED ... AS IDENTITY`, `SERIAL`, `AUTO_INCREMENT`, `DO $$`, `CREATE EXTENSION`, `CREATE ROLE`; only whitelisted statement types: `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT`, `INSERT` (seeds), `GRANT` | plan; user decision 1 | no |
| ANSI-004 | ANSI | PostgreSQL and H2 after `migrate` | query each catalog (`pg_trigger`/`pg_proc` for the schema; `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TRIGGERS`/`ROUTINES`) | zero triggers, zero routines, zero sequences owned by the schema | plan: completion criterion | yes |
| ANSI-005 | ANSI | every identifier in V files | check | lowercase, at most 30 characters, no quoted identifiers, no reserved words (`condition`, `release`, `user`, `type` renamed or checked against a reserved-word list) | AGS decision (identifiers lowercase, 30 chars) | no |
| ANSI-006 | ANSI | column types in V files | check | only from the allowed set (`VARCHAR(n)`, `CLOB`, `INTEGER`, `BIGINT`, `TIMESTAMP`, `DECIMAL`); no BOOLEAN/BIT, no `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE`, no `UUID`, `JSON(B)`, `TEXT`, `SERIAL` | user decisions 4,5 | no |
| ANSI-007 | ANSI | UNIQUE / PRIMARY KEY definitions | check | every column of a UNIQUE constraint is `NOT NULL` (normalised key columns `active_key`, `link_key`); no partial or expression index | plan: unique rule | no |
| ANSI-008 | ANSI | CHECK constraints in V files | check | no function calls, no regex; only IN-lists, comparisons, NULL-ness (matches REPO-004) | plan | no |
| ANSI-009 | ANSI | dynamic SQL builders in repositories | scan and run | values are always bind parameters; no string concatenation of caller input into SQL (also the injection corpus of REPO-027 and MCR-S06) | security | yes |
| ANSI-010 | ANSI | the checker itself | feed a corpus: each banned token alone, inside a comment, inside a string literal, as a substring of an identifier, in lower/upper/mixed case, split over lines | exactly the real uses are reported (mutation proof: removing a rule makes a seeded violation test fail; clean SQL has no false positive) | test-engineering | no |
| ANSI-011 | ANSI | candidate additional bans (proposed for ratification, not in the plan): `FETCH FIRST`, `OFFSET`, `\|\|` concatenation, `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`/`SYSDATE`/`GETDATE`, `LIKE` without `ESCAPE`, `LENGTH()` | run | reported as warnings listing hits; promoted to failures only after ratification | plan: time from `Clock` | no |
| ANSI-012 | ANSI | V files | check | no session/vendor settings (`SET ...`, `ALTER SESSION`, `PRAGMA`), no `COMMENT ON` | plan | no |
| ANSI-013 | ANSI | V files | run on PostgreSQL and H2 | the same text executes on both without a branch (no `dbms:`-style conditional, no per-DB file variant) | plan: no `dbms:` branch | yes |
| ANSI-014 | ANSI | every V file and every repository SQL string | scan | no `DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, `DEFAULT LOCALTIMESTAMP`, `DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE`, `DEFAULT NOW()`, `SYSDATE`, `GETDATE`, or any other DB-side time default or time function in SQL (`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, `LOCALTIMESTAMP`, `CURRENT_DATE`, `CURRENT_TIME`); catalogue check: no timestamp column has a column default; every time value is bound from the application UTC `Clock` | user decision: times from app UTC Clock | yes |

## 17. MIG: legacy import (`ledger-cli import-legacy`)

Source = the old PostgreSQL schema `ledger` as left by the old installer at `9fb62fe` (built in tests from the old `sql/*.sql` copied into the test resources, plus the data written by the fixtures below). Target = new schema; source and target may be the same PostgreSQL database or different (PG -> H2). The source connection is read-only.
| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MIG-001 | MIGRATION | rich source (project, task, defect incl. merged and UNRATED, defect_fix, defect_case, defect_task, defect_relation, condition, condition_link, session_grant incl. revoked/expired, history incl. IMPORT/LEGACY ops, request_receipt, common_code) | import | row count per table equals source; no extra rows; command prints counts and exit 0 | plan: 표별 행 수 | yes |
| MIG-002 | MIGRATION | same | per-table digest | SHA-256 over canonical row serialisation ordered by key (all columns, instants as UTC `Z`, empty-string rule of SVC-NORM applied identically on both sides and documented) equal source vs target; ids of history, fix, case, grant preserved (changed by import: the digest is the SHA-256 over the lines `<canonical key> TAB <SHA-256 of the canonical row> LF` ordered by the canonical key of the new table as a plain string comparison, so no collation of either DBMS takes part; the canonical row is `n;`, `s<length>:<text>;`, `l<number>;`, `t<UTC time, six fractional digits, Z>;` per column in the column order of the new table; the legacy side is the legacy row as mapped to the new table's shape (empty text as NULL, tokens in upper case, derived columns), the new side is the table read back; a test recomputes the digest of one table from this text alone) | plan: 요약값 | yes |
| MIG-003 | MIGRATION | history rows with `changes`, `legacy_ref`, IMPORT/LEGACY_NOTE/LEGACY_CHANGE, `actor_assurance` LEGACY_*, `login`, `request_id`, `corrects_id` | import | every field equal (JSON equality of `changes`); `corrects_id` references intact; **`before_data` is NULL on every imported row** (never fabricated) | user decision 3; plan | yes |
| MIG-004 | MIGRATION | request_receipt rows with stored responses | import | rows, responses and old `payload_sha256` kept; every imported row has `origin=LEGACY`; counts equal; a replay with any imported request id is refused with `REQUEST_ID_LEGACY` (SVC-RCP-15) and never returns the stored response or writes | plan; sql `_reserve`; decision 7i | yes |
| MIG-005 | MIGRATION | live, revoked and expired grants | import, then whoami and writes | (changed by import: grants are imported without a session key, so none is usable until the session's key is registered; the key-less write is MIG-030) the grants arrive with `active_key` set (`session\|project\|scope` for live and expired-but-unrevoked, `revoked:<id>` for revoked), `grant_scope` in upper case, `last_used_at` NULL; counts equal; `session_key` stays empty; once the key is registered whoami lists the live grants only (revoked and expired are not listed) | plan | yes |
| MIG-006 | MIGRATION | `created_by` values incl. `user:owner`, `unknown:legacy` and creators of re-linked rows | import | values copied as in the source (not recomputed): creators of defects, tasks, conditions, links; re-linked rows keep the current incarnation's creator; ambiguous legacy keys stay `unknown:legacy` | e2e.sh:S-U16..S-U21 (rewritten) | yes |
| MIG-007 | MIGRATION | defects up to `D-0100` non-contiguous plus merged ones; tasks `E2E-T-1`, `OLD-T-1` | import | project defect counter = maximum numeric suffix, task counter = maximum numeric suffix of `<CODE>-T-NNNN` ids (0 when none); next auto-assigned id is max+1 and collides with nothing; counters of `id_counter` for history, fix, case, grant = max existing id | plan: counters at import | yes |
| MIG-008 | MIGRATION | source schema | import | source untouched: per-table digests, object lists and `_migration` rows identical before and after; a write attempt on the source connection fails (read-only account) (H2: the fixture's second user has SELECT only, and the import's connections are recorded: `setReadOnly(true)` and isolation REPEATABLE READ before the first read, then commit; on PostgreSQL the account is the old `ledger_reader` login, test server stage for the DBMS part) | plan: old schema untouched | yes |
| MIG-009 | MIGRATION | import run twice | second run | refuses (target not empty) with exit 1; no duplicates | plan | yes |
| MIG-010 | MIGRATION | failure injected after table k | import | target has zero imported rows (single transaction) and a rerun succeeds (the failure is a test subclass of the write repository that throws when table k is written, after the earlier tables were written; the counters, the code revision and the audit trail are as before) | plan | yes |
| MIG-011 | MIGRATION | `category-legacy-fixture.json` (14 canonical categories) + a custom `OLDBUG` + severities + relation kinds | import | all `common_code` rows kept with state, sort order, ko and en texts; `CON` en name `Concurrency and atomicity`, `EXC` `Error cause suppression or substitution`; unknown category `OLDBUG` has English = Korean fallback; 14 known categories have English text different from Korean | e2e.sh:S-U11..S-U15 | yes |
| MIG-012 | MIGRATION | defect with severity `UNRATED` | import, then edit without severity; edit with `P3` | stays `UNRATED` until written; edit without rating `CODE_INVALID` (`defect.severity = UNRATED`); same edit with rating accepted, version 2 | e2e.sh:S-U22..S-U24 | yes |
| MIG-013 | MIGRATION | defect merged before the import | `ledger_defect(target)` | cases include `merged_behavior` of the merged defect with its observation text | e2e.sh:S-U25 | yes |
| MIG-014 | MIGRATION | links with the same target text in task and defect tables, ids containing `\|`, same-kind collisions | import | all rows imported without key collisions; `link_key` distinguishes kinds; nothing dropped | e2e.sh:S-U17..S-U20 | yes |
| MIG-015 | MIGRATION | 4000-character Korean text, quotes, newlines | import | digests equal (no truncation, no re-encoding) | REPO-031 | yes |
| MIG-016 | MIGRATION | `timestamptz` source values with sub-millisecond precision and mixed session zones | import | equal as instants; stored UTC; output ends in `Z` (H2 part: `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE` values with microseconds and mixed offsets, read as JDBC `OffsetDateTime`; the PostgreSQL `timestamptz` reading is test server stage) | user decision 4 | yes |
| MIG-017 | MIGRATION | merge chain where the child has a lower id than its target | import | succeeds (insert order or deferred constraint handled without DBMS-specific syntax); `merged_into` intact | 10_tables DEFERRABLE (replaced) | yes |
| MIG-018 | MIGRATION | after import | create, edit, transition, fix, verify, case on imported defects | version continues (`expected_version` from import), new history ids exceed all imported ids, no duplicate key anywhere | plan | yes |
| MIG-019 | MIGRATION | tampered target row after import | the command's verification step | exit 1 naming the table, count and digest (changed by import: the comparison is the last step of the import, inside its transaction, so a mismatch rolls the import back; `import-legacy --verify-only` repeats it later against what the new schema holds; tests: a row changed after the import, and a write side that changes a value during the import) | plan: 대조 | yes |
| MIG-020 | MIGRATION | source DSN password | run | taken from the secret store (the secret `ledger.import.source.password` is part of the environment through the configtree); never printed or logged (CLI-050, OutputSecrecyTest, `LegacySourceProperties.toString`) | plan | no |
| MIG-021 | MIGRATION | source without the `defect_case`/`common_code`/`created_by` migrations (older than `9fb62fe`), or missing schema | import | exit 1, message names the missing step; nothing written | e2e.sh:S-U01 (rewritten) | yes |
| MIG-022 | MIGRATION | `before_data`/`changes` of imported rows through the read tool | `ledger_history(include_before=true)` | `changes` present as imported, `before_data` null | user decision 3 | no |
| MIG-023 | MIGRATION | (a) source rows with `''` in nullable text columns; (b) source rows with `''` in NOT NULL text columns (`purpose`, `success_condition`, e.g. `OLD-T-UNKNOWN`) | import | (a) nullable columns normalised to NULL, and the digest comparison applies the same normalisation to both sides; (b) the import reports every offending row (table, key, column) on stdout, exits 1 and writes nothing (single transaction, SVC-NORM-02 stays valid); no value is invented to make the import pass; after the operator corrects the source rows the import succeeds | user decision 6; decision 7j | yes |
| MIG-028 | MIGRATION | source entity ids (defect, task, condition) containing a lower-case letter, and `\|`-containing ids such as `OLD-PIPE-C\|A` | import | upper-case ids with `\|` import unchanged; ids that violate the upper-case id regex are reported (table, key) and the import stops with exit 1 and no write, never lower/upper-cased silently (P: same stop-and-report rule as MIG-023; the plan does not state this case) | decision 7m; MIG-023 | yes |
| MIG-024 | MIGRATION | fixture builder | build source from `category-legacy-fixture.json`, old admin-written data (`old` project, OLD-D-0001..3, tasks, conditions, links, relinks, cases) | fixture loads in the source test database and is reused by MIG-001..023 and E2E-008 (a Java builder, `org.pgledger.testing.legacy.LegacyLedger` and `LegacyFixtures` in the test fixtures of the database module, no shell or PostgreSQL tool; it creates the table shapes of `sql/10_tables.sql` with the migrations 15, 16 and 17 applied in an ANSI/H2 rendering: `text` is `VARCHAR`, `jsonb` is `CLOB`, `uuid` is `UUID`, `timestamptz` is `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE`, identity columns take the fixture's ids; the old functions, views, triggers, regular-expression CHECKs, the partial index and the deferrable `merged_into` key are not rebuilt; a second H2 user has SELECT only and plays the read-only legacy login; the catalogue test checks the column lists against this shape) | e2e.sh `--upgrade-from` seed block | yes |
| MIG-025 | MIGRATION | PostgreSQL source -> H2 target and PostgreSQL -> PostgreSQL (same database, new schema) | MIG-001..003 | pass on both targets | plan; user decision 2 | test server stage (PostgreSQL): not run by the module tests; the source and target code is the same on H2 (MIG-001 .. MIG-003), the DBMS-specific reading (`jsonb` and `uuid` read as text, `timestamptz` as `OffsetDateTime`) needs the real DBMS |
| MIG-026 | MIGRATION | rollback | stop new services, restore old units | old data and old services unchanged (MIG-008 + E2E-015) (H2: MIG-008 covers the data; stopping and restoring the services is E2E-015, test server stage) | plan: rollback | no |
| MIG-027 | MIGRATION | old `timestamptz` values (including `2026-11-05 10:00:00.123456+00`, DST-ambiguous and KST-midnight instants) read with the old database session zone set to UTC, `Asia/Seoul` and `America/New_York` in turn | import | the epoch value (seconds + micros) of every imported timestamp equals the source epoch value (`EXTRACT(EPOCH ...)` computed on the source) in all three runs; target rows are identical across the three runs (H2 part: the fixture's `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE` values and `SET TIME ZONE` on the legacy session; the PostgreSQL `timestamptz` reading is test server stage) | user decision: UTC identical Instant | yes |
| MIG-029 | MIGRATION | defects that are merged into each other in a loop (A into B, B into A) | import | the defects are reported (table, key, column `merged_into`, reason `MERGE_CHAIN_LOOPS`) and nothing is written: the rows cannot be ordered so that every merge target is inserted first | MIG-017 (new by import) | yes |
| MIG-030 | MIGRATION | after the import: an author with imported grants and no registered session key; then the owner registers its key (what `key-register` does) | a write signed with an unregistered key; then a write by the author with its registered key | the first is refused with `ATTESTATION_SIGNATURE_INVALID` (no argument names the author, nothing is written, no receipt); the second works under the imported grant (the next id continues after the imported ones) | D-0012; plan: `import-legacy` moves the old grants without a key (new by import; replaces the key-less usability that MIG-005 expected) | yes |

| MIG-033 | MIGRATION | the legacy connection is configured, or not; a legacy schema name that is no identifier | ask the import service whether it is configured; build the source repository | `sourceConfigured` is true only with a source; the schema name must be lower case letters, digits and `_` (at most 30), otherwise `IllegalArgumentException` (the schema is an identifier and so never a bind value) | MIG-020 (new by import) | no |

## 18. Reconciliation: every old ID -> new ID or dropped

Disposition: **kept** = same behaviour, new mechanism; **rewritten: reason** = the old case asserted a PostgreSQL/DBHub/Node mechanism and is restated as the behaviour the user relies on; **dropped: reason**; **UNCLASSIFIED** = the plan and decisions do not say what happens (listed in 18.5, no guess made).

### 18.5 Open items after the second-round decisions
Items 1 to 13 of the former unclassified list were decided by the main agent (decision 7 in section 1.4) and are now concrete cases: setup-domain/nginx/cloudflared/SSL bundle (E2E-016..018), one-click wrapper (E2E-019..021), PostgreSQL dump before migrate (E2E-022), MCP health (ADM-045, ADM-046), CSRF (ADM-013, ADM-014; custom `Sec-Fetch-Site`/`Origin` checks dropped), FAILED schema file retry (SCH-005, SCH-020, SCH-021), driver registration yaml and setup mode (DRV-009, DRV-012, DRV-020..023, ADM-042), `match` ANY/ALL (MCR-S02), LEGACY receipts (MIG-004, SVC-RCP-15, REPO-041), legacy `''` (MIG-023, SVC-NORM-02), `category-add` dropped (CLI-006), three behaviours "unchanged by design" (DOM-019, SVC-FIX-16, SVC-COR-05), plan fixes (REPO-015, REPO-039, REPO-040, SVC-RACE-01, SVC-RACE-09, DOM-036, REPO-032, hook jar HK-001..HK-021, ARC-034).
Still unclassified or provisional (not decided, not guessed):
1. Generation of the Origin TLS certificate (the old script created a 10-year ECDSA certificate): decision 7a covers the SSL bundle configuration only; E2E-018 uses a test certificate and says nothing about who creates it in production.
2. Legacy entity ids that violate the upper-case id regex (MIG-028 states a stop-and-report rule marked P) and the exact regex constant (DOM-036 only requires that every id used by the old fixtures passes).
3. Names and keys marked `(P)` (read-tool input keys, `match`, error tokens of the registry) are provisional literals, not open behaviour.

Every list that has to mirror the code is derived from the code in the test (tools from `ToolContract`, commands and their syntax from `CliCommand` and the `usage.*` texts, modules from `settings.gradle.kts`, client directories from `clients/`, configuration keys from the `@ConfigurationProperties` declarations); nothing of that is copied. ## 20. INS: installer (task PG_LEDGER_MCP-T-0008, install stage)

Decisions this section applies (user, verbatim where quoted): the installers are the one non-Java exception, a thin `deploy/install.sh` (「아니다 2로 하자.」) and a thin `deploy/install.cmd` (「윈도우에도 깔수있잖아? 그러니 install.cmd 도 만들어줘.」), and everything else is `java -jar ledger-cli.jar <command>`; the product SQL is ANSI only, so creating the database accounts (a DBMS-specific statement) is an operator step with the DBMS's own tools before the installation, and the product applies only ANSI `GRANT`s (「쿼리는 ansi sql 방식으로 짜고, 특정 dbms 기능은 사용 금지야. 트리거도 사용 금지야.」); no Cloudflare dependency (「클라우드플레어에 종속이 안되었으면 좋겠어.」); macOS is not a target (「맥은 제외해도 돼」).
Design after 「애초에 개인피시에 깔면 서비스에 등록하는것만으로도 충분해. 그리고 os 권한분리? 대체 왜?」 and 「응 그렇게 하나로 줄여」: **one operating system service account** runs the MCP server and the admin web (Linux: the system user `pg-ledger-mcp`, never root; Windows: the predefined account LocalService), so that the secrets of the ledger (the admin token, the database password) are readable by it alone and not by the account of the person or the agents; **two database accounts**: the application account (both servers and the owner commands of `ledger-cli`, the least privilege of `db-templates/dml-grants.sql`: no DDL, no `UPDATE` or `DELETE` on the history) and the DDL account (`migrate`, `import-legacy`, the grants; its password is typed, hidden, and never stored).
Targets (user: 「개인 / 윈도우서버 / 리눅스서버」), told apart by render options of the Java tool and not by branches of the scripts: a **personal PC** is `install.cmd` with the defaults (loopback only, no reverse proxy, no TLS); a **Windows server** is `install.cmd` with public host names, behind a reverse proxy that the operator has prepared; a **Linux server** is `install.sh` (systemd) with the optional nginx site. A Linux desktop that is a personal PC follows the Linux procedure.
Tests run on H2 (an H2 `CREATE USER` is a test fixture only). The tests of the rendering and the scripts need no root, no Linux and no Windows.

| ID | Level | Given | When | Then | Source | DBMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INS-007 | REPO | the file `db-templates/dml-grants.sql` | read as text | only `GRANT` statements of the rights `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` on tables to `${dml_account}`: no `ALL`, `PUBLIC`, `GRANT OPTION`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `TRIGGER`; `${dml_account}` is the only placeholder | ANSI-001, user decision: ANSI only | no |
| INS-008 | REPO | `V*.sql` of the schema | collect every table | every table (and `schema_history`, made by the runner) is named in the template and no other table is; `history` never has `UPDATE` or `DELETE`, `schema_history` only `SELECT` (a table added by a later `V{n}` must be granted consciously: the test fails until it is) | brief: derived from code | no |
| INS-009 | REPO | `GrantApplier` | an account name with a quote, a space, a semicolon, a hyphen, a dot, a leading digit, an empty, a null or a 31-character name; a valid name | the invalid ones are refused before any statement runs (no SQL is built from them); a plain identifier is accepted | security: no SQL from user text | no |
| INS-010 | REPO | the owner account of H2 with the application account created by `CREATE USER` | apply the template twice; apply for an account that does not exist | the account can then read the ledger and the second application changes nothing; the unknown account is an `InfrastructureException` `SCHEMA_GRANT_FAILED` with the cause and no SQL in it. What the account can and cannot do (every path of the applications; no UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE on history, no DDL) is `DmlAccountTest` (REPO-023, REPO-024, REPO-038, SCH-014) run on the account granted by `GrantApplier` | idempotent re-run (upgrade path) | no |
| INS-011 | CLI | the real application over H2 (embedded profile), an empty database, the application account created | `migrate --app-account=<account>`; again; without the option; with a name that is not a plain account name, a blank option, an unknown option (`--mcp-account`); with an account that does not exist | the schema is migrated and then the template is applied (the account can read, and cannot change the history or the schema) and the output says so; a second run applies no file and grants again; without the option nothing is granted; a bad name is `INVALID_OPTION_VALUE`, a blank option `MISSING_OPTION_VALUE`, an unknown one `UNKNOWN_OPTION` (exit 2) and nothing is migrated; an unknown account is `GRANT_FAILED` (exit 1) with the schema migrated | design: grant application through the DDL account | no |

| INS-016 | INSTALL | `DeployRenderer` | render the settings | `application.yaml` has the JDBC URL and the one application account (and `ledger.schema.*` only when given); `mcp.yaml` and `admin.yaml` have the port and the allowed hosts (loopback and the given public host), the admin file also its driver file and driver directory and the health URL of the MCP server; no password, no token, no secret in any file; the files parse as YAML with the loader Spring Boot uses | design | no |
